k_{2}=\argmax_{k\in\{1,...,l\}}f^{k}(\mathbf{x})
.

A.2
Realizability Assumptions

Assumption 4

(Risk-based Realizability Assumption)
.

A domain space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
and hypothesis space
\mathcal{H}
satisfy the Risk-based Realizability Assumption, if for each domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, there exists at least one hypothesis function
h^{*}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
R_{D}(h^{*})=0
.

Assumption 5

(AUC-based Realizability Assumption)
.

A domain space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
and ranking function space
\mathcal{R}
satisfy the AUC-based Realizability Assumption under AUC, if for each domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, there exists at least one hypothesis function
r^{*}\in\mathcal{R}
such that
{\rm AUC}(h^{*};D_{XY})=1
.

A.3
Learnability and PAC learnability

Here we give a proof to show that Learnability given in Definition
1
and PAC learnability are equivalent.

First
, we prove that Learnability concludes the PAC learnability.

According to Definition
1
,

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)+\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n),

which implies that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}[R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)]\leq\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n).

Note that
R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)\geq 0
. Therefore, by Markov’s inequality, we have

\mathbb{P}(R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)<\epsilon)>1-\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}[R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)]/\epsilon\geq 1-\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)/\epsilon.

Because
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)
is monotonically decreasing, we can find a smallest
m
such that
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(m)\geq\epsilon\delta
and
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(m-1)<\epsilon\delta
, for
\delta\in(0,1)
. We define that
m(\epsilon,\delta)=m
. Therefore, for any
\epsilon>0
and
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a function
m(\epsilon,\delta)
such that when
n>m(\epsilon,\delta)
, with the probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)<\epsilon,

which is the definition of PAC learnability.

Second
, we prove that the PAC learnability concludes Learnability.

PAC-learnability: for any
\epsilon>0
and
0<\delta<1
, there exists a function
m(\epsilon,\delta)>0
such that when the sample size
n>m(\epsilon,\delta)
, we have that with the probability at least
1-\delta>0
,

R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)\leq\epsilon.

Note that the loss
\ell
defined in Section
2
has upper bound (because
\mathcal{Y}\cup\{K+1\}
is a finite set). We assume the upper bound of
\ell
is
M
. Hence, according to the definition of PAC-learnability, when the sample size
n>m(\epsilon,\delta)
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{S}[R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)]\leq\epsilon(1-\delta)+2M\delta<\epsilon+2M\delta.

If we set
\delta=\epsilon
, then when the sample size
n>m(\epsilon,\epsilon)
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{S}[R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)]<(2M+1)\epsilon,

this implies that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S}[R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)]=0,

which implies the Learnability in Definition
1
. We have completed this proof.

Appendix B
Proof of Theorem
1

See
1

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Proof of the First Result.

To prove that
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
is a priori-unknown space, we need to show that for any
D_{XY}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
, then
D_{XY}^{\alpha}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
.

According to the definition of
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
, for any
D_{XY}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
, we can find a domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, which can be written as
D_{XY}=(1-\pi^{\rm out})D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\pi^{\rm out}D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}
(here
\pi^{\rm out}\in[0,1)
) such that

D_{XY}^{\alpha^{\prime}}=(1-\alpha^{\prime})D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\alpha^{\prime}D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}.

Note that
D_{XY}^{\alpha}=(1-\alpha)D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\alpha D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}
. Therefore, based on the definition of
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
, for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
,
D_{XY}^{\alpha}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
, which implies that
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
is a prior-known space. Additionally, for any
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, we can rewrite
D_{XY}
as
D_{XY}^{\pi_{\rm out}}
, thus
D_{XY}=D_{XY}^{\pi_{\rm out}}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
, which implies that
\mathscr{D}_{XY}\subset\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\prime}
.

Proof of the Second Result.

First,
we prove that Definition
1
concludes Definition
2
, if
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
is a prior-unknown space:

\mathscr{D}_{XY}
is a priori-unknown space, and OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
.

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\Huge\Downarrow}

OOD detection is strongly learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
: there exist an algorithm
\mathbf{A}:\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, and a monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon(n)
, such that

\epsilon(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}\big[R^{\alpha}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))&-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\alpha}_{D}(h)\big]\leq\epsilon(n),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\alpha\in[0,1],\penalty\ \forall D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}.\end{split}

In the priori-unknown space, for any
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, we have that for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

D_{XY}^{\alpha}=(1-\alpha)D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\alpha D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}.

Then, according to the definition of learnability of OOD detection, we have an algorithm
\mathbf{A}
and a monotonically decreasing sequence

\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, such that for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D^{\alpha}}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D^{\alpha}}(h)+\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n),\penalty\ \penalty\ (\textnormal{{by\penalty\ the\penalty\ property\penalty\ of\penalty\ priori}-{\rm unknown\penalty\ space}})

where

R_{D^{\alpha}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}}\ell(\mathbf{A}(S)(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D^{\alpha}_{XY}(\mathbf{x},y),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ R_{D^{\alpha}}(h)=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D^{\alpha}_{XY}(\mathbf{x},y).

Since
R_{D^{\alpha}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}(S))
and
R_{D^{\alpha}}(h)=R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\alpha\in[0,1).

(10)

Next,
we consider the case that
\alpha=1
. Note that

\liminf_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)\geq\liminf_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h).

(11)

Then, we assume that
h_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{H}
satisfies that

R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{\epsilon})-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\leq\epsilon.

It is obvious that

R_{D}^{\alpha}(h_{\epsilon})\geq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h).

Let
\alpha\rightarrow 1
. Then, for any
\epsilon>0
,

R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{\epsilon})=\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h_{\epsilon})=\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h_{\epsilon})\geq\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h),

which implies that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{\epsilon})\geq\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)=\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h).

(12)

Combining Eq. (
11
) with Eq. (
12
), we have

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=\limsup_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)=\liminf_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h),

(13)

which implies that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h).

(14)

Note that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}(S))=(1-\alpha)\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}(S))+\alpha\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S)).

Hence, Lebesgue’s Dominated Convergence Theorem
(
Cohn 2013
)
implies that

\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}(S))=\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S)).

(15)

Using Eq. (
10
), we have that

\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\lim_{\alpha\rightarrow 1}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n).

(16)

Combining Eq. (
14
), Eq. (
15
) with Eq. (
16
), we obtain that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n).

Since
R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S))=R_{D}^{1}(\mathbf{A}(S))
and
R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=R_{D}^{1}(h)
, we obtain that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{1}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{1}(h)+\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n).

(17)

Combining Eq. (
10
) and Eq. (
17
), we have proven that: if the domain space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
is a priori-unknown space, then OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
.

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\Huge\Downarrow}

OOD detection is strongly learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
: there exist an algorithm
\mathbf{A}:\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, and a monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon(n)
, such that

\epsilon(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\alpha}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))&\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\alpha}_{D}(h)+\epsilon(n),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\alpha\in[0,1],\penalty\ \forall D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}.\end{split}

Second
, we prove that Definition
2
concludes Definition
1
:

OOD detection is strongly learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
: there exist an algorithm
\mathbf{A}:\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, and a monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon(n)
, such that

\epsilon(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}\big[R^{\alpha}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))&-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\alpha}_{D}(h)\big]\leq\epsilon(n),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall\alpha\in[0,1],\penalty\ \forall D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}.\end{split}

\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\Huge\Downarrow}

OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
.

If we set
\alpha=\pi^{\rm out}
, then
\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\alpha}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\alpha}_{D}(h)+\epsilon(n)
implies that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)+\epsilon(n),\end{split}

which means that OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
. We have completed this proof.

Proof of the Third Result.

The third result is a simple conclusion of the second result. Hence, we omit it.

Proof of the Fourth Result.

The fourth result is a simple conclusion of the property of AUC metric. Hence, we omit it.

∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
2

Before introducing the proof of Theorem
2
, we extend Condition
1
to a general version (Condition
5
). Then, Lemma
22
proves that Conditions
1
and
5
are the necessary conditions for the learnability of OOD detection. First, we provide the details of Condition
5
.

Let
\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}=\{(\lambda_{1},...,\lambda_{l}):\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}<1\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ \lambda_{j}\geq 0,\forall j=1,...,l\}
, where
l
is a positive integer. Next, we introduce an important definition as follows:

Definition 7

(OOD Convex Decomposition and Convex Domain)
.

Given any domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, we say joint distributions
Q_{1},...,Q_{l}
, which are defined over
\mathcal{X}\times\{K+1\}
, are the OOD convex decomposition for
D_{XY}
, if

D_{XY}=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}Q_{j},

for some
(\lambda_{1},...,\lambda_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
. We also say domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
is an OOD convex domain corresponding to OOD convex decomposition
Q_{1},...,Q_{l}
, if for any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
,

(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}Q_{j}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}.

We extend the linear condition (Condition
1
) to a multi-linear scenario.

Condition 5

(Multi-linear Condition)
.

For each OOD convex domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
corresponding to OOD convex decomposition
Q_{1},...,Q_{l}
, the following function

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l}):=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}

satisfies that

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),

where
\mathbf{0}
is the
1\times l
vector, whose elements are
0
, and
{\bm{\alpha}}_{j}
is the
1\times l
vector, whose
j
-th element is
1
and other elements are
0
.

This is a more general condition compared to Condition
1
. When
l=1
and the domain space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
is a priori-unknown space, Condition
5
degenerates into Condition
1
.
Lemma
22
shows that Condition
5
is necessary for the learnability of OOD detection.

Lemma 0
.

Given a priori-unknown space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
and a hypothesis space
\mathcal{H}
, if OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
, then Conditions
1
and
5
hold.

Proof of Lemma
22
.

Since Condition
1
is a special case of Condition
5
, we only need to prove that Condition
5
holds.

For any OOD convex domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
corresponding to OOD convex decomposition
Q_{1},...,Q_{l}
, and any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
, we set

Q^{\bm{\alpha}}=\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^{l}\alpha_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}Q_{j}.

Then, we define

D_{XY}^{\bm{\alpha}}=(1-\sum_{i=1}^{l}\alpha_{i})D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+(\sum_{i=1}^{l}\alpha_{i})Q^{\bm{\alpha}},\penalty\ \textnormal{which\penalty\ belongs\penalty\ to\penalty\ }\mathscr{D}_{XY}.

Let

R^{\bm{\alpha}}_{D}(h)=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D_{XY}^{\bm{\alpha}}(\mathbf{x},y).

Since OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{H}
, there exist an algorithm
\mathbf{A}:\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
, and a monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon(n)
, such that

\epsilon(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, and

0\leq\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\bm{\alpha}}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\bm{\alpha}}_{D}(h)\leq\epsilon(n).

Note that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\bm{\alpha}}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\rm in}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S)),\end{split}

and

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\bm{\alpha}}_{D}(h)=f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l}),

where

R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\{K+1\}}\ell(\mathbf{A}(S)(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}Q_{j}(\mathbf{x},y).

Therefore, we have that for any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
,

\begin{split}&\big|(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\rm in}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))-f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\big|\leq\epsilon(n).\end{split}

(18)

Let

g_{n}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\rm in}_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S)).

Note that Eq. (
18
) implies that

\begin{split}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})&=f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o},\\
\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}(\mathbf{0})&=f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0}).\end{split}

(19)

Step 1.
Since
{\bm{\alpha}}_{j}\notin\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
, we need to prove that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=f({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\textit{i.e.},\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j})=f({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),

(20)

where
{\bm{\alpha}}_{j}
is the
1\times l
vector, whose
j
-th element is
1
and other elements are
0
.

Let
\tilde{D}_{XY}=0.5*D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+0.5*Q_{j}
. The second result of Theorem
1
implies that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R^{\rm out}_{\tilde{D}}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R^{\rm out}_{\tilde{D}}(h)+\epsilon(n).\end{split}

Since
R^{\rm out}_{\tilde{D}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))
and
R^{\rm out}_{\tilde{D}}(h)=R_{Q_{j}}(h)
,

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)+\epsilon(n).

Note that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\leq\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))
. We have

0\leq\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\leq\epsilon(n).

(21)

Eq. (
21
) implies that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h).

(22)

We note that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)=f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j})
. Therefore,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{Q_{j}}(\mathbf{A}(S))=f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\penalty\ \textit{i.e.},\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j})=f({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}).

(23)

Step 2.
It is easy to check that for any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
,

\begin{split}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})&=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})g_{n}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}g_{n}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j})\big)\\
&=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}).\end{split}

(24)

According to Eq. (
19
) and Eq. (
23
), we have

\begin{split}&\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o},\\
&\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}(\mathbf{0})=f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0}),\\
&\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}g_{n}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j})=f({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\end{split}

(25)

Combining Eq. (
25
) with Eq. (
24
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 0
.

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)=(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h),\penalty\ \forall\alpha\in[0,1),

if and only if
for any
\epsilon>0
,

\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2\epsilon\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2\epsilon\}\neq\emptyset.

Proof of Lemma
23
.

For the sake of convenience, we set
f_{D}(\alpha)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)
, for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
.

First
, we prove that
f_{D}(\alpha)=(1-\alpha)f_{D}(0)+\alpha f_{D}(1)
,
\forall\alpha\in[0,1)
implies

\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2\epsilon\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2\epsilon\}\neq\emptyset.

For any
\epsilon>0
and
0\leq\alpha<1
, we can find
h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying that

R_{D}^{\alpha}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+\epsilon.

Note that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\alpha)R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\Big)\geq(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h).

Therefore,

(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)\leq R_{D}^{\alpha}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+\epsilon.

(26)

Note that
f_{D}(\alpha)=(1-\alpha)f_{D}(0)+\alpha f_{D}(1),\forall\alpha\in[0,1)
,
i.e.
,

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)=(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h),\forall\alpha\in[0,1).

(27)

Using Eqs. (
26
) and (
27
), we have that for any
0\leq\alpha<1
,

\epsilon\geq\big|R^{\alpha}_{D}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)\big|=\big|(1-\alpha)\big(R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)\big)+\alpha\big(R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\big)\big|.

(28)

Since
R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\geq 0
and
R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})-\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)\geq 0
, Eq. (
28
) implies that: for any
0<\alpha<1
,

\begin{split}&R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\epsilon/(1-\alpha),\\
&R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+\epsilon/\alpha.\end{split}

Therefore,

h_{\epsilon}^{\alpha}\in\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\epsilon/(1-\alpha)\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+\epsilon/\alpha\}.

If we set
\alpha=0.5
, we obtain that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2\epsilon\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2\epsilon\}\neq\emptyset.

Second
, we prove that for any
\epsilon>0
, if

\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2\epsilon\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2\epsilon\}\neq\emptyset,

then
f_{D}(\alpha)=(1-\alpha)f_{D}(0)+\alpha f_{D}(1)
, for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
.

Let
h_{\epsilon}\in\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2\epsilon\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2\epsilon\}
.

Then,

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)\leq R_{D}^{\alpha}(h_{\epsilon})\leq(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2\epsilon\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+2\epsilon,

which implies that
|f_{D}(\alpha)-(1-\alpha)f_{D}(0)-\alpha f_{D}(1)|\leq 2\epsilon
.

As
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
,
|f_{D}(\alpha)-(1-\alpha)f_{D}(0)-\alpha f_{D}(1)|\leq 0
. We have completed the proof.
∎

See
2

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Based on Lemma
22
, we obtain that
Condition
1
is the necessary condition for the learnability of OOD detection in the single-distribution space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{D_{XY}}
. Next, it suffices to prove that Condition
1
is the sufficient condition for the learnability of OOD detection in the single-distribution space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{D_{XY}}
.
We use Lemma
23
to prove the sufficient condition.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be the infinite sequence set that consists of all infinite sequences, whose coordinates are hypothesis functions,
i.e.
,

\mathscr{F}=\{{\bm{h}}=(h_{1},...,h_{n},...):\forall h_{n}\in\mathcal{H},n=1,....,+\infty\}.

For each
{\bm{h}}\in\mathscr{F}
, there is a corresponding algorithm
\mathbf{A}_{\bm{h}}
6
6

6

In this paper, we regard an algorithm as a mapping from
\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})^{n}
to
\mathcal{H}
or
\mathcal{R}
. So we can design an algorithm like this.
:
\mathbf{A}_{\bm{h}}(S)=h_{n},\penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ |S|=n
.
\mathscr{F}
generates an algorithm class
\mathscr{A}=\{\mathbf{A}_{\bm{h}}:\forall{\bm{h}}\in\mathscr{F}\}
. We select a consistent algorithm from the algorithm class
\mathscr{A}
.

We construct a special infinite sequence
\tilde{{\bm{h}}}=(\tilde{h}_{1},...,\tilde{h}_{n},...)\in\mathscr{F}
. For each positive integer
n
, we select
\tilde{h}_{n}
from
\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2/n\}\cap\{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}:R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{\prime})\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2/n\}
(the existence of
\tilde{h}_{n}
is based on Lemma
23
). It is easy to check that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}_{\tilde{{\bm{h}}}}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+2/n.\\
&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}_{\tilde{{\bm{h}}}}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+2/n.\end{split}

Since
(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm\alpha}(h)
, we obtain that for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}_{\tilde{{\bm{h}}}}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+2/n.\end{split}

We have completed this proof.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
3

See
3

Proof of Theorem
3
.

According to Definition
3
, we assume that
\mathbf{A}
is the AUC learnable algorithm: there exists learning rate
\epsilon(n)
such that for any
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
,

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}\big[\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY})-{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{XY})\big]\leq\epsilon(n).

(29)

For any
D_{XY}=\beta D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+(1-\beta)D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}},

D_{XY}^{\prime}=\beta^{\prime}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+(1-\beta^{\prime})D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, we set
{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha}=\alpha D_{X_{\rm O}}+(1-\alpha)D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}
. Then it is clear that for any
\tilde{\beta}\in(0,1]
,

\begin{split}&{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);\tilde{\beta}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+(1-\tilde{\beta})D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})\\
=&{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})\\
=&\alpha{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha){\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})\\
=&\alpha{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{XY})+(1-\alpha){\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{XY}^{\prime}).\end{split}

Therefore,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}\big[\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;\tilde{\beta}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+(1-\tilde{\beta})D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})-{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);\tilde{\beta}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+(1-\tilde{\beta})D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})\big]\\
\leq&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}\big[\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY}^{\prime})\\
&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ -\alpha{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{XY})-(1-\alpha){\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{XY}^{\prime})]\leq\epsilon(n),\end{split}

which implies that

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha}).

Note that

\begin{split}&\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})\\
=&\alpha\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm AUC}(\mathbf{A}(S);D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})\\
=&\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}).\end{split}

Therefore,

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})=\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}).

∎

Appendix E
Proofs of Theorem
4
and Theorem
5

E.1
Proof of Theorem
4

See
4

Proof of Theorem
4
.

We
first
explain how we get
f_{\rm I}
and
f_{\rm O}
in Definition
5
. Since
D_{X}
is absolutely continuous respect to
\mu
(
D_{X}\ll\mu
), then
D_{X_{\rm I}}\ll\mu
and
D_{X_{\rm O}}\ll\mu
. By Radon-Nikodym Theorem
(
Cohn 2013
)
, we know there exist two non-negative functions defined over
\mathcal{X}
:
f_{\rm I}

and
f_{\rm O}
such that for any
\mu
-measurable set
A\subset\mathcal{X}
,

D_{X_{\rm I}}(A)=\int_{A}f_{\rm I}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}),\penalty\ \penalty\ D_{X_{\rm O}}(A)=\int_{A}f_{\rm O}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}).

Second
, we prove that for any
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)>0
.

We define
A_{m}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:f_{\rm I}(\mathbf{x})\geq\frac{1}{m}\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ f_{\rm O}(\mathbf{x})\geq\frac{1}{m}\}
. It is clear that

\cup_{m=1}^{+\infty}A_{m}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:f_{\rm I}(\mathbf{x})>0\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ f_{\rm O}(\mathbf{x})>0\}=A_{\rm overlap},

and

A_{m}\subset A_{m+1}.

Therefore,

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\mu(A_{m})=\mu(A_{\rm overlap})>0,

which implies that there exists
m_{0}
such that

\mu(A_{m_{0}})>0.

For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we define

c_{\alpha}=\min_{y_{1}\in\mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}}\big((1-\alpha)\min_{y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell(y_{1},y_{2})+\alpha\ell(y_{1},K+1)\big).

It is clear that
c_{\alpha}>0
for
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then, for any
h\in\mathcal{H}
,

\begin{split}&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)\\
&=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D^{\alpha}_{XY}(\mathbf{x},y)\\
&=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}(1-\alpha)\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x},y)+\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\{K+1\}}\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x},y)\\
&\geq\int_{A_{m_{0}}\times\mathcal{Y}}(1-\alpha)\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x},y)+\int_{A_{m_{0}}\times\{K+1\}}\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x},y)\\
&=\int_{A_{m_{0}}}\big((1-\alpha)\int_{\mathcal{Y}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}D_{Y_{\rm I}|X_{\rm I}}(y|\mathbf{x})\big){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})\\
&+\int_{A_{m_{0}}}\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),K+1){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})\\
&\geq\int_{A_{m_{0}}}(1-\alpha)\min_{y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y_{2}){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})+\int_{A_{m_{0}}}\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),K+1){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})\\
&\geq\int_{A_{m_{0}}}(1-\alpha)\min_{y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y_{2})f_{\rm I}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x})+\int_{A_{m_{0}}}\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),K+1)f_{\rm O}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x})\\
&\geq\frac{1}{m_{0}}\int_{A_{m_{0}}}(1-\alpha)\min_{y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y_{2}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x})+\frac{1}{m_{0}}\int_{A_{m_{0}}}\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),K+1){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x})\\
&=\frac{1}{m_{0}}\int_{A_{m_{0}}}\big((1-\alpha)\min_{y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y_{2})+\alpha\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),K+1)\big){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x})\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m_{0}}\mu(A_{m_{0}})>0.\end{split}

Therefore,

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)\geq\frac{c_{\alpha}}{m_{0}}\mu(A_{m_{0}})>0.

Third
, Condition
1
indicates that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)=(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=0
(here we have used conditions
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=0
and
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=0
), which contradicts with
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)>0
(
\alpha\in(0,1)
). Therefore, Condition
1
does not hold. Using Lemma
22
, we obtain that OOD detection in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
is not learnable for
\mathcal{H}
.
∎

E.2
Proof of Theorem
5

See
5

Proof of Theorem
5
.

We need to prove that OOD detection is not learnable in the total space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{\rm all}
for
\mathcal{H}
, if
\mathcal{H}
is non-trivial,
i.e.
,

\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:\exists h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{H},\textnormal{s.t.}\penalty\ h_{1}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{Y},h_{2}(\mathbf{x})=K+1\}\neq\emptyset.
The main idea is to construct a domain
D_{XY}
satisfying that:

1) the ID and OOD distributions have overlap (Definition
5
);

2)
R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{1})=0
,
R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{2})=0
.

According to the condition that
\mathcal{H}
is non-trivial, we know that there exist
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
h_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{1})\in\mathcal{Y},h_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{1})=K+1
, for some
\mathbf{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}
. We set
D_{XY}=0.5*\delta_{(\mathbf{x}_{1},h_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{1}))}+0.5*\delta_{(\mathbf{x}_{1},h_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{1}))}
, where
\delta
is the Dirac measure. It is easy to check that
R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{1})=0
,
R_{D}^{\rm out}(h_{2})=0
, which implies that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=0
and
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=0
. In addition, the ID distribution
\delta_{(\mathbf{x}_{1},h_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{1}))}
and OOD distribution
\delta_{(\mathbf{x}_{1},h_{2}(\mathbf{x}_{1}))}
have overlap
\mathbf{x}_{1}
.
By using Theorem
4
, we have completed this proof.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
6

Before proving Theorem
6
, we need three important lemmas.

Lemma 0
.

Suppose that
D_{XY}
is a domain with OOD convex decomposition
Q_{1},...,Q_{l}
(convex decomposition is given by Definition
7
in Appendix
C
), and
D_{XY}
is a finite discrete distribution, then (the definition of
f_{D,Q}
is given in Condition
5
)

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o},

if and only if

\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h),

where
\mathbf{0}
is the
1\times l
vector, whose elements are
0
, and
{\bm{\alpha}}_{j}
is the
1\times l
vector, whose
j
-th element is
1
and other elements are
0
, and

R_{Q_{j}}(h)=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\{K+1\}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),y){\rm d}Q_{j}(\mathbf{x},y).

Proof of Lemma
24
.

To better understand this proof, we recall the definition of
f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})
:

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}

First
, we prove that if

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o},

then,

\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h).

Let
D_{XY}=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}Q_{j}
, for some
(\lambda_{1},...,\lambda_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
. Since
D_{XY}
has finite support set, we have

\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big)\neq\emptyset.

We can find that
h_{0}\in\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big)
. Hence,

(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h_{0})=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big).

(30)

Note that the condition
f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j})
implies

(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big).

(31)

Therefore, Eq. (
30
) and Eq. (
31
) imply that

\begin{split}(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h_{0}).\end{split}

(32)

Since
R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{0})\geq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)
and
R_{Q_{j}}(h_{0})\geq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}^{\rm in}(h)
, for
j=1,...,l
, then using Eq. (
32
), we have that

\begin{split}&R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{0})=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h),\\
&R_{Q_{j}}(h_{0})=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall j=1,...,l,\end{split}

which implies that

h_{0}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h).

Therefore,

\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)\subset\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h).

(33)

Additionally, using

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o},

we obtain that for any
h^{\prime}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)
,

\begin{split}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=&\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big)\\
=&(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\\
=&(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\prime})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\lambda_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h^{\prime})=R_{D}(h^{\prime}),\end{split}

which implies that

h^{\prime}\in\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h).

Therefore,

\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)\subset\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h).

(34)

Combining Eq. (
33
) with Eq. (
34
), we obtain that

\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h).

Second
, we prove that if

\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h),

then,

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \forall(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}.

We set

h_{0}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h),

then, for any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
,

\begin{split}(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)&\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big)\\
&\leq(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h_{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h_{0})\\
&=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h).\end{split}

Therefore, for any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
,

\begin{split}&(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{Q_{j}}(h)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\Big((1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}R_{Q_{j}}(h)\Big),\end{split}

which implies that: for any
(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})\in\Delta_{l}^{\rm o}
,

f_{D,Q}(\alpha_{1},...,\alpha_{l})=(1-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j})f_{D,Q}(\mathbf{0})+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\alpha_{j}f_{D,Q}({\bm{\alpha}}_{j}).

We have completed this proof.
∎

Lemma 0
.

Suppose that Assumption
1
holds.
If there is a finite discrete domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
such that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}({\bm{h}})>0,
then OOD detection is not learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
.

Proof of Lemma
25
.

Suppose that
{\rm supp}D_{X_{\rm O}}=\{\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\rm out},...,\mathbf{x}_{l}^{\rm out}\}
, then it is clear that
D_{XY}
has OOD convex decomposition
\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\rm out}},...,\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{l}^{\rm out}}
, where
\delta_{\mathbf{x}}
is the dirac measure whose support set is
\{\mathbf{x}\}
.

Since
\mathcal{H}
is the separate space for OOD (
i.e.
, Assumption
1
holds), then
\forall j=1,...,l
,

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)=0,

where

R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),K+1){\rm d}\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}(\mathbf{x}).

This implies that: if
\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)\neq\emptyset
, then for
\forall h^{\prime}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)
,

h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\rm out})=K+1,\penalty\ \forall i=1,...,l.

Therefore, if
\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)\neq\emptyset
,

then for any
h^{*}\in\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)
, we have that

h^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\rm out})=K+1,\penalty\ \forall i=1,...,l.

Proof by Contradiction
: assume OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
, then Lemmas
22
and
24
imply that

\bigcap_{j=1}^{l}\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\rm out}}}(h)\bigcap\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)\neq\emptyset.

Therefore, for any
h^{*}\in\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)
, we have that

h^{*}(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\rm out})=K+1,\penalty\ \forall i=1,...,l,

which implies that for any
h^{*}\in\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)
, we have

R_{D}^{\rm out}(h^{*})=0,

which implies that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=0
.
It is clear that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=0
is
inconsistent
with the condition
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)>0
. Therefore,
OOD detection is not learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
.
∎

Lemma 0
.

If Assumption
1
holds,

{\rm VCdim}(\phi\circ\mathcal{H})=v<+\infty
and
\sup_{{h}\in\mathcal{H}}|\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:{h}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{Y}\}|>m
such that
v<m
, then
OOD detection is not learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
, where
\phi
maps ID’s labels to
{1}
and maps OOD’s labels to
2
.

Proof of Lemma
26
.

Due to
\sup_{{h}\in\mathcal{H}}|\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:{\bm{h}}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{Y}\}|>m
, we can obtain a set

C=\{\mathbf{x}_{1},...,\mathbf{x}_{m},\mathbf{x}_{m+1}\},

which satisfies that there exists
\tilde{h}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\tilde{h}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\in\mathcal{Y}
for any
i=1,...,m,m+1
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\phi}=\{(\phi\circ h(\mathbf{x}_{1}),...,\phi\circ h(\mathbf{x}_{m}),\phi\circ h(\mathbf{x}_{m+1}):h\in\mathcal{H}\}
. It is clear that

(1,1,...,1)=(\phi\circ\tilde{h}(\mathbf{x}_{1}),...,\phi\circ\tilde{h}(\mathbf{x}_{m}),\phi\circ\tilde{h}(\mathbf{x}_{m+1}))\in\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\phi},

where
(1,1,...,1)
means all elements are
1
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{m+1}^{\phi}=\{(\phi\circ h(\mathbf{x}_{1}),...,\phi\circ h(\mathbf{x}_{m}),\phi\circ h(\mathbf{x}_{m+1}):h\penalty\ \textnormal{is\penalty\ any\penalty\ hypothesis\penalty\ function\penalty\ from\penalty\ }\mathcal{X}\penalty\ \textnormal{to}\penalty\ \mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}\}
.

Clearly,
\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\phi}\subset\mathcal{H}_{m+1}^{\phi}
and
|\mathcal{H}_{m+1}^{\phi}|=2^{m+1}
. Sauer-Shelah-Perles Lemma (Lemma 6.10 in
(
Shalev-Shwartz and Ben-David 2014
)
) implies that

|\mathcal{H}^{\phi}_{C}|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{v}\tbinom{m+1}{i}.

Since
\sum_{i=0}^{v}\tbinom{m+1}{i}<2^{m+1}-1
(because
v<m
), we obtain that
|\mathcal{H}^{\phi}_{C}|\leq 2^{m+1}-2
. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}^{\phi}_{C}\cup\{(2,2...,2)\}
is a proper subset of
\mathcal{H}_{m+1}^{\phi}
, where
(2,2,...,2)
means that all elements are
2
. Note that
(1,1...,1)
(all elements are 1) also belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{C}^{\phi}
. Hence,
\mathcal{H}^{\phi}_{C}\cup\{(2,2...,2)\}\cup\{(1,1...,1)\}
is a proper subset of
\mathcal{H}_{m+1}^{\phi}
, which implies that we can obtain a hypothesis function
h^{\prime}
satisfying that:

\begin{split}&1)(\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{1}),...,\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{m}),\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{m+1}))\notin\mathcal{H}^{\phi}_{C};\\
&2)\textnormal{\penalty\ There\penalty\ exist}\penalty\ \mathbf{x}_{j},\mathbf{x}_{p}\in C\textnormal{\penalty\ such \penalty\ that}\penalty\ \phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{j})=2\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ \phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{p})=1.\end{split}

Let
C_{\rm I}=C\cap\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=1\}
and
C_{\rm O}=C\cap\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=2\}
.

Then, we construct a special domain
D_{XY}
:

D_{XY}=0.5*D_{X_{\rm I}}*D_{Y_{\rm I}|X_{\rm I}}+0.5*D_{X_{\rm O}}*D_{Y_{\rm O}|X_{\rm O}},\penalty\ \textnormal{where}

D_{X_{\rm I}}=\frac{1}{|C_{\rm I}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm I}}\delta_{\mathbf{x}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ D_{Y_{\rm I}|X_{\rm I}}(y|\mathbf{x})=1,\penalty\ \textnormal{if}\penalty\ \penalty\ \tilde{h}(\mathbf{x})=y\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm I};

and

D_{X_{\rm O}}=\frac{1}{|C_{\rm O}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm O}}\delta_{\mathbf{x}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ D_{Y_{\rm O}|X_{\rm O}}(K+1|\mathbf{x})=1,\penalty\ \textnormal{if}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm O}.

Since
D_{XY}
is a finite discrete distribution and
(\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{1}),...,\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{m}),\phi\circ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{m+1}))\notin\mathcal{H}^{\phi}_{C}
, it is clear that
\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)\neq\emptyset
and
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)>0
.
Additionally,
R_{D}^{\rm in}(\tilde{h})=0
. Therefore,
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=0
.

Proof by Contradiction
: suppose that OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
, then Lemma
22
implies that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=0.5*\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+0.5*\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h).

Therefore, if OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
, then
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)>0
.

Until now, we have constructed a domain
D_{XY}
(defined over
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}_{\rm all}
) with finite support and satisfying that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)>0
. Note that
\mathcal{H}
is the separate space for OOD data (Assumption
1
holds). Using Lemma
25
, we know that OOD detection is not learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
, which is
inconsistent
with our assumption that OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
. Therefore, OOD detection is not learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
. We have completed the proof.
∎

See
6

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Let
{\rm VCdim}(\phi\circ\mathcal{H})=v
. Since
\sup_{{h}\in\mathcal{H}}|\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:{\bm{h}}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{Y}\}|=+\infty
, it is clear that
\sup_{{h}\in\mathcal{H}}|\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:{\bm{h}}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{Y}\}|>v
. Using Lemma
26
, we complete this proof.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs of Lemma
7
and Theorem
8

G.1
Proof of Lemma
7

Definition 8
.

Given a ranking function space
\mathcal{R}
, the corresponding hypothesis space
\mathcal{G}
consists of all
g_{r}
satisfying that there exists a
r\in\mathcal{R}
such that

g_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})={\rm sign}(r(\mathbf{x})-r(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})).

Definition 9

(Equivalence)
.

Given measures
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
, we say two ranking functions
r\in\mathcal{R}
and
r^{\prime}\in\mathcal{R}
are AUC equivalent over
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
, i.e.,
f\sim f^{\prime}
w.r.t.
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
, if and only if the corresponding hypothesis functions
g_{f}=g_{f^{\prime}}
a.e.
\mu_{1}\times\mu_{2}
.

Lemma 0
.

Assume that
D_{X_{\rm I}}=\int g_{X_{\rm I}}{\rm d}\mu
and
D_{X_{\rm O}}=\int g_{X_{\rm O}}{\rm d}\mu
, then

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(f;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\sim\mu}\max\{g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})\}.

Proof.

Let
D(X)=\int g{\rm d}\mu
,
D_{X_{\rm I}}=\int g_{X_{\rm I}}{\rm d}\mu
and
D_{X_{\rm O}}=\int g_{X_{\rm O}}{\rm d}\mu
satisfying that
g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}=2g
. Let
P=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:g(\mathbf{x})>0\}
,
P_{X_{\rm I}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})>0\}
and
P_{X_{\rm O}}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})>0\}
. To any two points
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
, we consider that

\begin{split}R(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})&=\big[\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x})>r(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x})=r(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big]g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\\
&+\big[\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})>r(\mathbf{x})}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x})=r(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\big]g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x}).\end{split}

We set
r^{*}(\mathbf{x})={\rm sigmoid}\big({g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})}/{g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})}\big)
, if
g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})>0
; otherwise,
r^{*}(\mathbf{x})=1
.
It is clear that
we have that

R(r^{*},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\max_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}R(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\max\{g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})\}.

Due to

2{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=\int_{P}\int_{P}R(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),

then

\begin{split}&2{\rm AUC}(r^{*};D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=\int_{P}\int_{P}\max_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}R(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\\
\geq&\max_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}\int_{P}\int_{P}R(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mu(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\max_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}2{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}).\end{split}

Therefore,
r^{*}
is the optimal solution, and

{\rm AUC}(r^{*};D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\sim\mu}\max\{g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),g_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})\}.

We have completed this proof.
∎

Lemma 0
.

Given a ranking function space
\mathcal{R}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}
,
D_{X_{\rm I}}=\int g_{X_{\rm I}}{\rm d}\mu
,
D_{X_{\rm O}}=\int g_{X_{\rm O}}{\rm d}\mu
and
D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}=\int g_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}{\rm d}\mu
,
if

\begin{split}\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}),\\
\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})=\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}),\end{split}

and there exists
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that

\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})=\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha}),

where
{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha}=\alpha D_{X_{\rm O}}+(1-\alpha)D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}
.
Then

\frac{g_{X_{\rm I}}}{g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}}\sim\frac{g_{X_{\rm I}}}{g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}}\penalty\ \text{w.r.t.}\penalty\ \penalty\ D_{X_{\rm I}}|_{P_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}-P_{X_{\rm O}}},D_{X_{\rm I}}|_{P_{X_{\rm O}}-P_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}},

where
P_{X_{\rm O}}=\{\mathbf{x}:g_{X_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x})>0\}
and
P_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}=\{\mathbf{x}:g_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})>0\}

Proof.

Let
\eta_{X_{\rm I}}=\frac{g_{X_{\rm I}}}{g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}}
and
\eta_{X_{\rm I}}^{\prime}=\frac{g_{X_{\rm I}}}{g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}}
.
It is easy to check that the proof process of Lemma
27
implies that to each
i=1,2
,

\eta_{X_{\rm I}}^{i}\in\argmax_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(f;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{i}).

Additionally,

\begin{split}\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})=&\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})\\
=&\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})\\
\geq&\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})\geq\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha}).\end{split}

Therefore, under the condition that

\begin{split}&\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}),\\
&\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})=\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}),\end{split}

we obtain that

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},{D}_{X_{\rm O}}^{\alpha})=\alpha\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})+(1-\alpha)\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}).

Because
\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})
and
\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})
are attainable (the proof process of Lemma
27
implies this), it is easy to check (similar the proof of Lemma
24
) that

\argmax_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})\cap\argmax_{r\in\mathcal{R}_{\rm all}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset.

Combining with Lemma
27
, above equality implies there exists
r^{*}
such that

r^{*}\sim\eta_{X_{\rm I}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{w.r.t.}\penalty\ D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ r^{*}\sim\eta_{X_{\rm I}}^{\prime},\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{w.r.t.}\penalty\ D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}

Therefore,

\eta_{X_{\rm I}}\sim\eta_{X_{\rm I}}^{\prime}\penalty\ \text{w.r.t.}\penalty\ D_{X_{\rm I}}|_{P_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}-P_{X_{\rm O}}},D_{X_{\rm I}}|_{P_{X_{\rm O}}-P_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}}

We have completed the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7
.

By the condition that
D_{X_{\rm I}}(P_{1}\cap P_{2})<\min\{D_{X_{\rm I}}(P_{1}),D_{X_{\rm I}}(P_{2})\}
, we can ensure that

D_{X_{\rm I}}(P_{1}-P_{2})>0,\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ D_{X_{\rm I}}(P_{2}-P_{1})>0.

By this, one can easily check that

\frac{g_{X_{\rm I}}}{g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}}\sim\frac{g_{X_{\rm I}}}{g_{X_{\rm I}}+g_{X_{\rm O}}^{\prime}}\penalty\ \text{w.r.t.}\penalty\ D_{X_{\rm I}}|_{P_{2}-P_{1}},D_{X_{\rm I}}|_{P_{1}-P_{2}}\penalty\ \text{does not hold.}

Therefore, Lemma
28
implies that Condition
2
does not hold.

Let
D_{X_{\rm I}}=\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\mathbf{x}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}
,

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
9

Lemma 0
.

Given a separate ranking function space
\mathcal{R}
, if there exists finite discrete
D_{XY}\subset\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
such that

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY})<1,

then OOD detection is not learnable under AUC in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{R}
.

Proof of Lemma
29
.

Assume that if OOD detection is learnable under AUC in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
for
\mathcal{R}
, then Condition
2
implies that: let
D_{X_{\rm O}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}
(
\sum_{i=1}^{m}=1
),

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{i}}).

Because
\mathcal{R}
is the separate ranking function space, we know that

\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},\delta_{\mathbf{x}_{i}})=1,

which is conflict with the condition that
\sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{XY})<1
. We have completed this proof.
∎

See
9

Proof of Theorem
9
.

Given disjoint samples
\mathbf{X}=\{\mathbf{x}_{1},...,\mathbf{x}_{m}\}
. Consider the following matrix and set

\mathbf{B}_{r}[\mathbf{X}]=\big[\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x}_{i})>r(\mathbf{x}_{j})}\big],\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{B}_{\mathcal{R}}[\mathbf{X}]=\{\mathbf{B}_{r}[\mathbf{X}]:r\in\mathcal{R}\}.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\rm I},X_{\rm O}}[\mathbf{X}]
be the set consisting of all
(D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})
which satisfies the following conditions:

•

D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}
are from the separate space;

•

D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}}
are uniform distributions;

•

{\rm supp}D_{X_{\rm I}}\cup{\rm supp}D_{X_{\rm O}}=\mathbf{X}
.

Additionally, let

\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}[\mathbf{X}]=\{(D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})\in\mathcal{D}_{X_{\rm I},X_{\rm O}}[\mathbf{X}]:\exists r\in\mathcal{R},{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})=1\}.

It is clear that

|\mathbf{B}_{\mathcal{R}}[\mathbf{X}]|\leq\frac{e^{d}}{d^{d}}m^{2d};\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ |\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}[\mathbf{X}]|\leq(m-1)|\mathbf{B}_{\mathcal{R}}[\mathbf{X}]|\leq\frac{e^{d}}{d^{d}}m^{2d+1};\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ |\mathcal{D}_{X_{\rm I},X_{\rm O}}[\mathbf{X}]|=2^{m}-2.

When
m
is large enough (
m\geq(28d+14)\log(14d+7)
), we have that

|\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{R}}[\mathbf{X}]|<|\mathcal{D}_{X_{\rm I},X_{\rm O}}[\mathbf{X}]|.

Therefore, we can find

(D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})\in\mathcal{D}_{X_{\rm I},X_{\rm O}}[\mathbf{X}]\penalty\ \text{such\penalty\ that}\penalty\ \sup_{r\in\mathcal{R}}{\rm AUC}(r;D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})<1.

By Lemma
29
, we have completed this proof.
∎

Appendix I
Proofs of Theorem
10
and Theorem
11

I.1
Proof of Theorem
10

Firstly, we need two lemmas, which are motivated by Lemma 19.2 and Lemma 19.3 in
(
Shalev-Shwartz and Ben-David 2014
)
.

Lemma 0
.

Let
C_{1}
,…,
C_{r}
be a cover of space
\mathcal{X}
, i.e.,
\sum_{i=1}^{r}C_{i}=\mathcal{X}
. Let
S_{X}=\{\mathbf{x}^{1},...,\mathbf{x}^{n}\}
be a sequence of
n
data drawn from
D_{X_{\rm I}}
, i.i.d. Then

\mathbb{E}_{S_{X}\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\Big(\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\Big)\leq\frac{r}{en}.

Proof of Lemma
30
.

\mathbb{E}_{S_{X}\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\Big(\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\Big)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Big(D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\cdot\mathbb{E}_{S_{X}\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}\big)\Big),

where
\mathbf{1}
is the characteristic function.

For each
i
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S_{X}\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\big(\mathbf{1}_{C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}\big)&=\int_{\mathcal{X}^{n}}\mathbf{1}_{C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}{\rm d}D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}(S_{X})\\
&=\big(\int_{\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{1}_{C_{i}\cap\{\mathbf{x}\}=\emptyset}{\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x})\big)^{n}\\
&=\big(1-D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\big)^{n}\leq e^{-nD_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})}.\end{split}

Therefore,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S_{X}\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\Big(\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S=\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\Big)&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{r}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})e^{-nD_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})}\\
&\leq r\max_{i\in\{1,...,r\}}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})e^{-nD_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})}\leq\frac{r}{ne},\end{split}

here we have used inequality:
\max_{i\in\{1,...,r\}}a_{i}e^{-na_{i}}\leq 1/{(ne)}
. The proof has been completed.
∎

Lemma 0
.

Let
K=1
. When
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a bounded set, there exists a monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(m)
satisfying that
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(m)\rightarrow 0
, as
m\rightarrow 0
, such that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}},S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))<\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n),

where
{\rm dist}
is the Euclidean distance,
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S)=\argmin_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\in S_{X}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})
, here
S_{X}
is the feature part of
S
, i.e.,
S_{X}=\{\mathbf{x}^{1},...,\mathbf{x}^{n}\}
, if
S=\{(\mathbf{x}^{1},y^{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}^{n},y^{n})\}
.

Proof of Lemma
31
.

Since
\mathcal{X}
is bounded, without loss of generality, we set
\mathcal{X}\subset[0,1)^{d}
. Fix
\epsilon=1/T
, for some integer
T
. Let
r=T^{d}
and
C_{1},C_{2},...,C_{r}
be a cover of
\mathcal{X}
: for every
(a_{1},...,a_{T})\in[T]^{d}:=[1,...,T]^{d}
, there exists a
C_{i}=\{\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},...,x_{d}):\forall j\in\{1,...,d\},x_{j}\in[(a_{j}-1)/T,a_{j}/T)\}
.

If
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
belong to some
C_{i}
, then
{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\leq\sqrt{d}\epsilon
; otherwise,
{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\leq\sqrt{d}
. Therefore,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}},S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))\\
\leq&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}\Big(\sqrt{d}\epsilon\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}\neq\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})+\sqrt{d}\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\Big)\\
\leq&\mathbb{E}_{S_{X}\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\Big(\sqrt{d}\epsilon\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}\neq\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})+\sqrt{d}\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}=\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\Big).\end{split}

Note that
C_{1},...,C_{r}
are disjoint. Therefore,
\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}\neq\emptyset}D_{X_{\rm I}}(C_{i})\leq D_{X_{\rm I}}(\sum_{i:C_{i}\cap S_{X}\neq\emptyset}C_{i})\leq 1
. Using Lemma
30
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}},S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))\leq\sqrt{d}\epsilon+\frac{r\sqrt{d}}{ne}=\sqrt{d}\epsilon+\frac{\sqrt{d}}{ne{\epsilon}^{d}}.\end{split}

If we set
\epsilon=2n^{-1/(d+1)}
, then

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}},S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))\leq\frac{2\sqrt{d}}{n^{1/(d+1)}}+\frac{\sqrt{d}}{2^{d}en^{1/(d+1)}}.\end{split}

If we set
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)=\frac{2\sqrt{d}}{n^{1/(d+1)}}+\frac{\sqrt{d}}{2^{d}en^{1/(d+1)}}
, we complete this proof.
∎

See
10

Proof of Theorem
10
.

First
, we prove that if the hypothesis space
\mathcal{H}
is a separate space for OOD (
i.e.
, Assumption
1
holds), the constant function
h^{\rm in}:=1\in\mathcal{H}
, then that OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
implies
\mathcal{H}_{\rm all}-\{h^{\rm out}\}\subset\mathcal{H}
.

Proof by Contradiction
: suppose that there exists
h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{\rm all}
such that
h^{\prime}\neq h^{\rm out}
and
h^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{H}
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=\{\mathbf{x}_{1},...,\mathbf{x}_{m}\}
,
C_{\rm I}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{Y}\}
and
C_{\rm O}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}:h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=K+1\}
.

Because
h^{\prime}\neq h^{\rm out}
, we know that
C_{\rm I}\neq\emptyset
.

We construct a special domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
: if
C_{\rm O}=\emptyset
, then
D_{XY}=D_{X_{\rm I}}*D_{Y_{\rm I}|X_{\rm I}}
; otherwise,

D_{XY}=0.5*D_{X_{\rm I}}*D_{Y_{\rm I}|X_{\rm I}}+0.5*D_{X_{\rm O}}*D_{Y_{\rm O}|X_{\rm O}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{where}

D_{X_{\rm I}}=\frac{1}{|C_{\rm I}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm I}}\delta_{\mathbf{x}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ D_{Y_{\rm I}|X_{\rm I}}(y|\mathbf{x})=1,\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{if}\penalty\ h^{\prime}(\mathbf{x})=y\penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm I},

and

D_{X_{\rm O}}=\frac{1}{|C_{\rm O}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm O}}\delta_{\mathbf{x}}\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{and}\penalty\ \penalty\ D_{Y_{\rm O}|X_{\rm O}}(K+1|\mathbf{x})=1,\penalty\ \penalty\ \textnormal{if}\penalty\ \mathbf{x}\in C_{\rm O}.

Since
h^{\prime}\notin\mathcal{H}
and
|\mathcal{X}|<+\infty
, then
\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)\neq\emptyset
, and
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)>0
. Additionally,
R_{D}^{\rm in}(h^{\rm in})=0
(here
h^{\rm in}=1
), hence,
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=0
.

Since OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
, Lemma
22
implies that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)=(1-\pi^{\rm out})\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\pi^{\rm out}\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h),

where
\pi^{\rm out}=D_{Y}(Y=K+1)=1
or
0.5
.
Since
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=0
and
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)>0
, we obtain that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)>0
.

Until now, we have constructed a special domain
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
satisfying that
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)>0
. Using Lemma
25
, we know that OOD detection in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
is not learnable for
\mathcal{H}
, which is
inconsistent
with the condition that OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
. Therefore, the assumption (there exists
h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{\rm all}
such that
h^{\prime}\neq h^{\rm out}
and
h\notin\mathcal{H}
) doesn’t hold, which implies that
\mathcal{H}_{\rm all}-\{h^{\rm out}\}\subset\mathcal{H}
.

Second
, we prove that if
\mathcal{H}_{\rm all}-\{h^{\rm out}\}\subset\mathcal{H}
, then OOD detection is learnable in
\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
for
\mathcal{H}
.

To prove this result, we need to design a special algorithm. Let
d_{0}=\min_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}\penalty\ {\rm and}\penalty\ {\mathbf{x}\neq\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
, where
{\rm dist}
is the Euclidean distance. It is clear that
d_{0}>0
. Let

\mathbf{A}(S)(\mathbf{x})=\left\{\begin{aligned} \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ 1,&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))<0.5*d_{0};\\
\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ 2,&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))\geq 0.5*d_{0},\\
\end{aligned}\right.

where
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S)=\argmin_{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\in S_{X}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})
,
here
S_{X}
is the feature part of
S
,
i.e.
,
S_{X}=\{\mathbf{x}^{1},...,\mathbf{x}^{n}\}
, if
S=\{(\mathbf{x}^{1},y^{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}^{n},y^{n})\}
.

For any
\mathbf{x}\in{\rm supp}D_{X_{\rm I}}
, it is easy to check that for almost all
S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}
,

{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))>0.5*d_{0},

which implies that

\mathbf{A}({S})(\mathbf{x})=2,

hence,

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S))=0.

(35)

Using Lemma
31
, for any
\mathbf{x}\in{\rm supp}D_{X_{\rm I}}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}},S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))<\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n),

where
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow 0
and
\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)
is a monotonically decreasing
sequence.

Hence, we have that

D_{X_{\rm I}}\times D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}^{n}(\{(\mathbf{x},S):{\rm dist}(\mathbf{x},\pi_{1}(\mathbf{x},S))\geq 0.5*d_{0}\})\leq 2\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)/d_{0},

where
D_{X_{\rm I}}\times D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}^{n}
is the product measure of
D_{X_{\rm I}}
and
D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}^{n}

(
Cohn 2013
)
.
Therefore,

D_{X_{\rm I}}\times D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}^{n}(\{(\mathbf{x},S):\mathbf{A}(S)(\mathbf{x})=1\})>1-2\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)/d_{0},

which implies that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq 2B\epsilon_{\rm cons}(n)/d_{0},

(36)

where
B=\max\{\ell(1,2),\ell(2,1)\}
. Using Eq. (
35
) and Eq. (
36
), we have proved that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}^{n}}R_{D}(\mathbf{A}(S))\leq 0+2B\epsilon_{\rm cons}(m)/d_{0}\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}(h)+2B\epsilon_{\rm cons}(m)/d_{0}.

(37)

It is easy to check that
\mathbf{A}(S)\in\mathcal{H}_{\rm all}-\{h^{\rm out}\}
. Therefore, we have constructed a consistent algorithm
\mathbf{A}
for
\mathcal{H}
. We have completed this proof.
∎

I.2
Proof of Theorem
11

See
11

Proof of Theorem
11
.

Since
|\mathcal{X}|<+\infty
, we know that
|\mathcal{H}|<+\infty
, which implies that
\mathcal{H}^{\rm in}
is agnostic PAC learnable for supervised learning in classification. Therefore, there exist an algorithm
\mathbf{A}^{\rm in}:\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\rm in}
and a monotonically decreasing
sequence
\epsilon(n)
, such that
\epsilon(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, and for any
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
,

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm in}(S))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm in}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\epsilon(n).

Since
|\mathcal{X}|<+\infty
and
\mathcal{H}^{\rm b}
almost contains all binary classifiers, then using Theorem
10
and Theorem
1
, we obtain that there exist an algorithm
\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}:\cup_{n=1}^{+\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\{1,2\})^{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\rm b}
and a monotonically decreasing sequence
\epsilon^{\prime}(n)
, such that

\epsilon^{\prime}(n)\rightarrow 0
, as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, and for any
D_{XY}\in\mathscr{D}_{XY}^{s}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm b}}R_{\phi(D)}^{\rm in}(h)+\epsilon^{\prime}(n),\\
&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm b}}R_{\phi(D)}^{\rm out}(h)+\epsilon^{\prime}(n),\end{split}

where
\phi
maps ID’s labels to
1
and OOD’s label to
2
,

R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}\ell(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S))(\mathbf{x}),\phi(y)){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x},y),

(38)

R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm in}(h)=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),\phi(y)){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}(\mathbf{x},y),

(39)

R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\{K+1\}}\ell(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S))(\mathbf{x}),\phi(y)){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x},y),

(40)

and

R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm out}(h)=\int_{\mathcal{X}\times\{K+1\}}\ell(h(\mathbf{x}),\phi(y)){\rm d}D_{X_{\rm O}Y_{\rm O}}(\mathbf{x},y),

(41)

here
\phi(S)=\{(\mathbf{x}^{1},\phi({y}^{1})),...,(\mathbf{x}^{n},\phi({y}^{n}))\}
, if
S=\{(\mathbf{x}^{1},{y}^{1}),...,(\mathbf{x}^{n},{y}^{n})\}
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}^{b}
almost contains all classifiers, and
\mathscr{D}^{s}_{XY}
is the separate space. Hence,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))\leq\epsilon^{\prime}(n),\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))\leq\epsilon^{\prime}(n).\end{split}

Next
, we construct an algorithm
\mathbf{A}
using
\mathbf{A}^{\rm in}
and
\mathbf{A}^{\rm out}
.

\mathbf{A}(S)(\mathbf{x})=\left\{\begin{aligned} K+1,&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S))(\mathbf{x})=2;\\
\mathbf{A}^{\rm in}(S)(\mathbf{x}),&\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S))(\mathbf{x})=1.\\
\end{aligned}\right.

Since
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{\phi(D)}^{\rm in}(\phi\circ h)=0
,
\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)=0
, then by Condition
3
, it is easy to check that

\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm in}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h).

Additionally, the risk
R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}(S))
is from two parts: 1) ID data are detected as OOD data; 2) ID data are detected as ID data, but are classified as incorrect ID classes. Therefore, we have the inequality:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}(S))&\leq\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm in}(S))+c\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm in}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))\\
&\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}^{\rm in}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\epsilon(n)+c\epsilon^{\prime}(n)=\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\epsilon(n)+c\epsilon^{\prime}(n),\end{split}

(42)

where
c=\max_{y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}}\ell(y_{1},y_{2})/\min\{\ell(1,2),\ell(2,1)\}
.

Note that the risk
R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S))
is from the case that OOD data are detected as ID data. Therefore,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}(S))&\leq c\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{|rmI}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{\phi({D})}^{\rm out}(\mathbf{A}^{\rm b}(\phi(S)))\\
&\leq c\epsilon^{\prime}(n)\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)+c\epsilon^{\prime}(n).\end{split}

(43)

Note that
(1-\alpha)\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm in}(h)+\alpha\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\rm out}(h)\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)
. Then, using Eq. (
42
) and Eq. (
43
), we obtain that for any
\alpha\in[0,1]
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}Y_{\rm I}}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{A}(S))&\leq\inf_{h\in\mathcal{H}}R_{D}^{\alpha}(h)+\epsilon(n)+c\epsilon^{\prime}(n).\end{split}

According to Theorem
1
(the second result), we complete the proof.
∎

Appendix J
Proof of Theorem
12

Lemma 0
.

Suppose that
|\mathcal{X}|<+\infty
. If AUC-based Realizability Assumption holds for AUC metric, then OOD detection is learnable under AUC in separate space for
\mathcal{R}
.

Proof of Lemma
32
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
K=1
, and any
r\in\mathcal{R}
satisfies that
0<r<1
(one can achieve this by using sigmoid function). Given
m
data points
S_{m}=\{\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\prime},...,\mathbf{x}_{m}^{\prime}\}\subset\mathcal{X}^{m}
. We consider the following learning rule

\max_{r\in\mathcal{R},\tau\in(0,1)}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime})\leq\tau}\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\notin S},\penalty\ \text{subject\penalty\ to}\penalty\ \frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}_{r(\mathbf{x}_{j})\leq\tau}=0.

We denote the algorithm, which solves the above rule, as
\mathbf{A}_{S_{m}}
(
\mathbf{A}_{S_{m}}
outputs
r
and a corresponding
\tau
). For different data points
S_{m}
, we have different algorithm
\mathbf{A}_{S_{m}}
. Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set that consists of all data points,
i.e.
,

\mathcal{S}:=\{S_{m}:S_{m}\penalty\ \textnormal{are any}\penalty\ m\textnormal{ data points},\penalty\ m=1,...,+\infty\}.

(44)

Using
\mathcal{S}
, we construct an algorithm space as follows:

\mathscr{A}:=\{\mathbf{A}_{{S}^{\prime}}:\forall\penalty\ S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}\}.

We will find an algorithm
\mathbf{A}
from
\mathscr{A}
, which is learnable. Let
S^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}
. We will show that
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
can guarantee the learnability. Suppose that
r_{S}
and
\tau_{S}
is the output of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{X}}(S)
, then the realizability assumption implies that there exists learning rate
\epsilon(n)
such that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{S}(\mathbf{x})\leq\tau_{S}}\leq\epsilon(n).

(45)

Additionally, due to
{\rm supp}(D_{X_{\rm O}})\subset\mathcal{X}-S
, the AUC-based Realizability Assumption implies that

\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D^{n}_{X_{\rm I}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm O}}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{S}(\mathbf{x})\leq\tau_{S}}=1.

(46)

Then,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}^{n}}{\rm AUC}(f_{S};D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})\\
\geq&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm O}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{S}(\mathbf{x})<r_{S}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\\
\geq&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}\sim D_{X_{\rm O}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{S}(\mathbf{x})\leq\tau_{S}}\mathbf{1}_{r_{S}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})>\tau_{S}}\\
\geq&1-\epsilon(n).\end{split}

Then the ranking function part
r_{S}
of
\mathbf{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\in\mathscr{A}
is the universally consistent algorithm,
i.e.
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{S\sim D_{X_{\rm I}}^{n}}{\rm AUC}(r_{S};D_{X_{\rm I}},D_{X_{\rm O}})\geq 1-\epsilon(n).\end{split}

∎

See
12

Proof of Theorem
12
.

Lemmas
29
and
32
imply this result.
∎

Appendix K
Proofs of Theorems
13
and
14

K.1
Proof of Theorem
13

Lemma 0
.

Given a prior-unknown space
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
and a hypothesis space
\mathcal{H}
, if Condition
4
holds, then for any equivalence class
[D_{XY}^{\prime}]
with respect to
\mathscr{D}_{XY}
, OOD detection is learnable in the equivalence class
[D_{XY}^{\prime}]
for
\mathcal{H}
. Furthermore, the learning rate can attain
O(1/n)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{F}
be a set consisting of all infinite sequences, whose coordinates are hypothesis functions,
i.e.
,

\mathscr{F}=\{{\bm{h}}=(h_{1},...,h_{n},...):\forall h_{n}\in\mathcal{H},n=1,....,+\infty\}.

For each
{\bm{h}}\in\mathscr{F}
, there is a corresponding algorithm
\mathbf{A}_{\bm{h}}
:
\mathbf{A}_{\bm{h}}(S)=h_{n},\penalty\ {\rm if}\penalty\ |S|=n
.
\mathscr{F}
generates an algorithm class
